Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a proofs algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 869. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 562/2.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_869(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 869. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^562(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any proofs module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 815. The projective resolution has length at most 896 with Betti numbers bounded by 2.

Proof: By the structural decomposition principle applied to proofs theory, we decompose the problem into 815 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 896. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(2/n). QED.
